===== FILE: .github\skills\ami-firmware-coding-standards\Firmware_Coding_Standards.md  (924 lines) =====
00001| # AMI Aptio 5.x Firmware Coding Standards (INFORMATIONAL)
00002| 
00003| **Source:** AMI Aptio 5.x Firmware Coding Standards  
00004| **Compliance Level:** INFORMATIONAL - Style recommendations only  
00005| **Scope:** Firmware-specific style guidance extending Module Developer's Guide
00006| 
00007| ---
00008| 
00009| ## Validation Scope: AMI Code Only (INFORMATIONAL)
00010| 
00011| These standards are **INFORMATIONAL** style recommendations for AMI-owned code. Violations do NOT impact the final review verdict.
00012| 
00013| ### Relationship to Module Developer's Guide
00014| 
00015| | Standard | Compliance Level | Impact |
00016| |----------|------------------|--------|
00017| | **Module Developer's Guide** | MANDATORY | Violations impact verdict (MAJOR/MINOR) |
00018| | **Firmware Coding Standards** (this document) | INFORMATIONAL | Violations tracked for post-merge improvement |
00019| 
00020| **All Firmware Coding Standards findings use INFO severity.**
00021| 
00022| ---
00023| 
00024| ## 1. Comment Style and Documentation
00025| 
00026| ### Function Header Comments
00027| 
00028| **Guideline:** All functions should have complete Doxygen-style header comments.
00029| 
00030| ```c
00031| /**
00032|   Initialize the NVRAM driver.
00033| 
00034|   This function performs the following:
00035|   - Allocates NVRAM buffers
00036|   - Registers variable services protocol
00037|   - Installs NVRAM access protocol
00038| 
00039|   @param[in]  ImageHandle   The firmware allocated handle for the EFI image
00040|   @param[in]  SystemTable   A pointer to the EFI System Table
00041| 
00042|   @retval EFI_SUCCESS       NVRAM initialized successfully
00043|   @retval EFI_OUT_OF_RESOURCES  Failed to allocate buffers
00044|   @retval EFI_DEVICE_ERROR  NVRAM hardware error
00045| 
00046| **/
00047| EFI_STATUS
00048| EFIAPI
00049| NvramDxeInit (
00050|   IN EFI_HANDLE        ImageHandle,
00051|   IN EFI_SYSTEM_TABLE  *SystemTable
00052|   )
00053| ```
00054| 
00055| **Observations (INFO severity):**
00056| - Missing `@param` tags
00057| - Missing `@retval` descriptions
00058| - Missing function purpose description
00059| - Missing detailed behavior notes
00060| 
00061| ### Inline Comments
00062| 
00063| **Guideline:** Complex logic should have explanatory comments.
00064| 
00065| ✅ **Good:**
00066| ```c
00067| // Check if variable is authenticated before allowing delete
00068| if (!(Attributes & EFI_VARIABLE_AUTHENTICATED_WRITE_ACCESS)) {
00069|   return EFI_SECURITY_VIOLATION;
00070| }
00071| ```
00072| 
00073| ⚠️ **Needs Improvement:**
00074| ```c
00075| // Check flags
00076| if (!(Attr & 0x20)) {  // INFO: Magic number, unclear purpose
00077|   return EFI_SECURITY_VIOLATION;
00078| }
00079| ```
00080| 
00081| ### TODO/FIXME/HACK Markers
00082| 
00083| **Guideline:** Use standardized markers for known issues.
00084| 
00085| ```c
00086| // TODO: Optimize buffer allocation for S3 path
00087| // FIXME: Race condition when multiple threads access the variable
00088| // HACK: Temporary workaround for silicon errata, remove in next release
00089| ```
00090| 
00091| ---
00092| 
00093| ## 2. Source File Structure
00094| 
00095| ### C File Organization
00096| 
00097| **Guideline:** C source files must follow this strict order:
00098| 
00099| 1. **File Header** (copyright + `@file` block)
00100| 2. **Include Statements** (grouped and ordered)
00101| 3. **Constants, Macros, and Type Definitions**
00102| 4. **Variable and External Declarations**
00103| 5. **Function Definitions**
00104| 
00105| **Rules:**
00106| - Function prototypes/declarations must appear before function definitions
00107| - Group related declarations together
00108| - No mixed ordering (e.g., function definition → variable → function definition)
00109| - Function definitions should appear in logical order (initialization → helpers → main logic)
00110| 
00111| ✅ **Good Structure:**
00112| ```c
00113| //***********************************************************************
00114| // File Header
00115| //***********************************************************************
00116| 
00117| // Include(s)
00118| #include <Uefi.h>
00119| #include <Library/BaseLib.h>
00120| 
00121| // Constant Definition(s)
00122| #define MAX_SIZE  256
00123| 
00124| // Type Definition(s)
00125| typedef struct {
00126|   UINT32  Value;
00127| } MY_STRUCT;
00128| 
00129| // Function Prototype(s)
00130| EFI_STATUS HelperFunction(VOID);
00131| EFI_STATUS MainFunction(VOID);
00132| 
00133| // Variable Declaration(s)
00134| STATIC UINT32  mCounter = 0;
00135| 
00136| // Function Definition(s)
00137| EFI_STATUS
00138| HelperFunction (
00139|   VOID
00140|   )
00141| {
00142|   // Implementation
00143| }
00144| 
00145| EFI_STATUS
00146| MainFunction (
00147|   VOID
00148|   )
00149| {
00150|   // Implementation
00151| }
00152| ```
00153| 
00154| ⚠️ **Poor Structure (INFO):**
00155| ```c
00156| // Function definition before declaration
00157| EFI_STATUS SomeFunction(VOID) { ... }  // INFO: Declare before define
00158| 
00159| // Variable after function
00160| STATIC UINT32 mVar;  // INFO: Variables should come before functions
00161| 
00162| // Another function
00163| EFI_STATUS AnotherFunction(VOID) { ... }
00164| ```
00165| 
00166| **Observations (INFO severity):**
00167| - Mixed ordering of declarations and definitions
00168| - Function definitions before declarations
00169| - Variables scattered throughout file
00170| - Related functions not grouped
00171| 
00172| ---
00173| 
00174| ## 3. Header File Rules
00175| 
00176| ### No Code in Headers
00177| 
00178| **Guideline:** Header files (`.h`) must contain ONLY declarations, never code implementations.
00179| 
00180| ❌ **Prohibited in Headers:**
00181| ```c
00182| // MyHeader.h
00183| void MyFunction() {  // INFO: Function definition in header
00184|   DoSomething();
00185| }
00186| 
00187| STATIC UINT32 mVariable = 0;  // INFO: Variable definition in header
00188| ```
00189| 
00190| ✅ **Correct:**
00191| ```c
00192| // MyHeader.h
00193| void MyFunction(VOID);  // Declaration only
00194| 
00195| extern UINT32 mVariable;  // External declaration
00196| ```
00197| 
00198| ### Include Guards
00199| 
00200| **Guideline:** All header files must use include guards.
00201| 
00202| ✅ **Required Pattern:**
00203| ```c
00204| #ifndef _MY_HEADER_H_
00205| #define _MY_HEADER_H_
00206| 
00207| // Header content
00208| 
00209| #endif  // _MY_HEADER_H_
00210| ```
00211| 
00212| **Observations (INFO severity):**
00213| - Missing include guards
00214| - Incorrect guard naming (should match filename)
00215| - Missing closing `#endif` comment
00216| 
00217| ### Self-Contained Headers
00218| 
00219| **Guideline:** Headers should be self-contained and include all dependencies.
00220| 
00221| ✅ **Good:**
00222| ```c
00223| // MyProtocol.h
00224| #include <Uefi.h>  // Needed for EFI_STATUS
00225| 
00226| EFI_STATUS InstallMyProtocol(VOID);
00227| ```
00228| 
00229| ⚠️ **Needs Improvement:**
00230| ```c
00231| // MyProtocol.h
00232| EFI_STATUS InstallMyProtocol(VOID);  // INFO: Missing <Uefi.h> for EFI_STATUS
00233| ```
00234| 
00235| ### Minimize Header Includes
00236| 
00237| **Guideline:** Include only what is directly needed in the header.
00238| 
00239| - Avoid including headers that are only needed by the `.c` implementation
00240| - Use forward declarations when possible
00241| - Separate public vs private definitions (use separate headers if needed)
00242| 
00243| ✅ **Good:**
00244| ```c
00245| // MyPublic.h - Public interface
00246| typedef struct _MY_PROTOCOL MY_PROTOCOL;  // Forward declaration
00247| 
00248| EFI_STATUS InstallProtocol(MY_PROTOCOL *Protocol);
00249| ```
00250| 
00251| ---
00252| 
00253| ## 4. Source Line Formatting
00254| 
00255| ### One Statement Per Line
00256| 
00257| **Guideline:** Each statement should be on its own line.
00258| 
00259| ❌ **Avoid:**
00260| ```c
00261| Status = Init(); if (EFI_ERROR(Status)) return Status;  // INFO: Split into multiple lines
00262| ```
00263| 
00264| ✅ **Good:**
00265| ```c
00266| Status = Init();
00267| if (EFI_ERROR(Status)) {
00268|   return Status;
00269| }
00270| ```
00271| 
00272| ### Operator Spacing
00273| 
00274| **Guideline:** Consistent spacing around operators improves readability.
00275| 
00276| ✅ **Good Spacing:**
00277| ```c
00278| Result = Value1 + Value2;       // Space around binary operators
00279| Pointer->Member;                // No space around ->
00280| Structure.Field;                // No space around .
00281| Array[Index];                   // No space before [
00282| Function(Arg1, Arg2);           // Space after comma
00283| for (i = 0; i < Max; i++);      // Space after semicolon
00284| ```
00285| 
00286| ⚠️ **Inconsistent (INFO):**
00287| ```c
00288| Result=Value1+Value2;           // INFO: Add spaces
00289| Pointer -> Member;              // INFO: Remove spaces
00290| Function(Arg1,Arg2);            // INFO: Add space after comma
00291| ```
00292| 
00293| ### Variable Declarations
00294| 
00295| **Guideline:** One variable per line (except related simple variables).
00296| 
00297| ✅ **Preferred:**
00298| ```c
00299| UINT32  BufferSize;
00300| UINT32  MaxSize;
00301| UINT8   *Buffer;
00302| ```
00303| 
00304| ⚠️ **Avoid:**
00305| ```c
00306| UINT32 BufferSize, MaxSize, *Pointer;  // INFO: Split into separate lines
00307| ```
00308| 
00309| **Observations (INFO severity):**
00310| - Multiple variables declared on one line
00311| - Inconsistent spacing in declarations
00312| - Mixed types on same line
00313| 
00314| ---
00315| 
00316| ## 5. Naming Quality
00317| 
00318| ### Meaningful Names
00319| 
00320| **Guideline:** Use descriptive, unambiguous names that convey purpose.
00321| 
00322| ❌ **Avoid Ambiguous Names:**
00323| ```c
00324| UINT32  Size1, Size2;           // INFO: What do these sizes represent?
00325| UINT32  Var1, Var2, Var3;       // INFO: Non-descriptive
00326| UINT32  Temp, Tmp, T;           // INFO: Overly abbreviated
00327| ```
00328| 
00329| ✅ **Better:**
00330| ```c
00331| UINT32  InputBufferSize;
00332| UINT32  OutputBufferSize;
00333| UINT32  MaxTransferSize;
00334| ```
00335| 
00336| ### Avoid Similar Names
00337| 
00338| **Guideline:** Prevent confusion from similar-looking identifiers.
00339| 
00340| ⚠️ **Confusing (INFO):**
00341| ```c
00342| UINT32  BufferSize;             // Lower camel case
00343| UINT32  buffersize;             // All lowercase - INFO: Too similar
00344| UINT32  BUFFERSIZE;             // All caps - INFO: Case-only difference
00345| UINT32  Buffer_Size;            // Underscore variant - INFO: Inconsistent
00346| ```
00347| 
00348| **Observations (INFO severity):**
00349| - Case-only differences between identifiers
00350| - Similar names with minor variations
00351| - Single-letter differences (e.g., `DataPtr` vs `DataPrt`)
00352| 
00353| ### Appropriate Abbreviations
00354| 
00355| **Guideline:** Use well-known abbreviations; avoid cryptic short forms.
00356| 
00357| ✅ **Acceptable Abbreviations:**
00358| - `Max`, `Min`, `Num`, `Ptr`, `Addr`, `Len`, `Idx`
00359| - `Init`, `Cfg`, `Tmp`, `Mgr`, `Ctrl`
00360| 
00361| ⚠️ **Overly Abbreviated (INFO):**
00362| ```c
00363| UINT32  BufSz;                  // INFO: Use BufferSize
00364| UINT32  NmElmnts;               // INFO: Use NumElements
00365| EFI_STATUS  Sts;                // INFO: Use Status
00366| ```
00367| 
00368| **Observations (INFO severity):**
00369| - Unclear abbreviations
00370| - Inconsistent abbreviation style within same file
00371| - Names too short to be meaningful
00372| 
00373| ---
00374| 
00375| ## 6. Third-Party Code Modification (Porting Markers)
00376| 
00377| ### AMI Porting Markers - REQUIRED
00378| 
00379| **Guideline:** ALL modifications to third-party or EDK2 code MUST be wrapped with AMI porting markers.
00380| 
00381| **Required Pattern:**
00382| ```c
00383| //*** AMI PORTING BEGIN ***
00384| // Modified code here
00385| //*** AMI PORTING END ***
00386| ```
00387| 
00388| **Applies to:**
00389| - EDK2 packages (`MdePkg`, `MdeModulePkg`, `SecurityPkg`, `CryptoPkg`, `UefiCpuPkg`)
00390| - Intel packages (`Intel/`)
00391| - Any third-party code not owned by AMI
00392| 
00393| ✅ **Correct Usage:**
00394| ```c
00395| // Original Intel/EDK2 code
00396| void OriginalFunction()
00397| {
00398|   // Original implementation
00399|   
00400|   //*** AMI PORTING BEGIN ***
00401|   // AMI-specific validation added
00402|   if (!ValidateAmiRequirement()) {
00403|     return EFI_SECURITY_VIOLATION;
00404|   }
00405|   //*** AMI PORTING END ***
00406|   
00407|   // Rest of original code
00408| }
00409| ```
00410| 
00411| ❌ **Missing Markers (INFO):**
00412| ```c
00413| // Modified third-party code without markers
00414| void ThirdPartyFunction()
00415| {
00416|   // Original code
00417|   CustomAmiFunction();  // INFO: AMI modification not marked
00418|   // Original code
00419| }
00420| ```
00421| 
00422| ### Marker Placement Rules
00423| 
00424| 1. **Wrap only AMI changes** - Do not include original code inside markers
00425| 2. **One marker pair per change** - Separate distinct modifications
00426| 3. **Add brief description** - Explain why modification was needed
00427| 4. **Keep markers minimal** - Only changed lines, not entire functions
00428| 
00429| ✅ **Good:**
00430| ```c
00431| Status = AllocateBuffer();
00432| if (EFI_ERROR(Status)) {
00433|   //*** AMI PORTING BEGIN ***
00434|   // Added logging for debug tracking
00435|   DEBUG((DEBUG_ERROR, "[AMI] Buffer allocation failed: %r\n", Status));
00436|   //*** AMI PORTING END ***
00437|   return Status;
00438| }
00439| ```
00440| 
00441| ### Alternative Marker Names
00442| 
00443| These are also acceptable (project-specific):
00444| - `//*** AMI PORTING BEGIN ***` / `//*** AMI PORTING END ***` (preferred)
00445| - `// AMI OVERRIDE START` / `// AMI OVERRIDE END`
00446| - `//APTIOV_SERVER_OVERRIDE_START:` / `//APTIOV_SERVER_OVERRIDE_END:`
00447| 
00448| **Observation (INFO severity):**
00449| - Modifications to third-party code without porting markers
00450| - Incorrect marker syntax
00451| - Missing explanation in marker comments
00452| - Markers wrapping too much unmodified code
00453| 
00454| ---
00455| 
00456| ## 7. Code Readability
00457| 
00458| ### Line Length
00459| 
00460| **Guideline:** Prefer lines under 120 characters.
00461| 
00462| **Observation:** Lines exceeding 120 characters reduce readability (INFO severity).
00463| 
00464| ✅ **Good:**
00465| ```c
00466| Status = gBS->InstallMultipleProtocolInterfaces(
00467|                 &Handle,
00468|                 &gEfiNvramAccessProtocolGuid,
00469|                 &mNvramAccessProtocol,
00470|                 NULL
00471|                 );
00472| ```
00473| 
00474| ⚠️ **Consider Refactoring:**
00475| ```c
00476| Status = gBS->InstallMultipleProtocolInterfaces(&Handle, &gEfiNvramAccessProtocolGuid, &mNvramAccessProtocol, &gEfiNvramControlProtocolGuid, &mNvramControlProtocol, NULL);  // INFO: Line too long
00477| ```
00478| 
00479| ### Function Length
00480| 
00481| **Guideline:** Functions should be under 200 lines.
00482| 
00483| **Observation:** Functions exceeding 200 lines suggest refactoring opportunities (INFO severity).
00484| 
00485| **Suggestion:** Break into smaller, focused functions:
00486| - Extract complex logic into helper functions
00487| - Separate initialization from processing
00488| - Create subfunctions for error handling paths
00489| 
00490| ### Nesting Depth
00491| 
00492| **Guideline:** Prefer nesting depth under 5 levels.
00493| 
00494| ⚠️ **Deep Nesting (INFO):**
00495| ```c
00496| if (Condition1) {
00497|   if (Condition2) {
00498|     if (Condition3) {
00499|       if (Condition4) {
00500|         if (Condition5) {
00501|           // INFO: Consider refactoring (5+ levels deep)
00502|         }
00503|       }
00504|     }
00505|   }
00506| }
00507| ```
00508| 
00509| ✅ **Better - Early Returns:**
00510| ```c
00511| if (!Condition1) return EFI_INVALID_PARAMETER;
00512| if (!Condition2) return EFI_NOT_READY;
00513| if (!Condition3) return EFI_DEVICE_ERROR;
00514| if (!Condition4) return EFI_ACCESS_DENIED;
00515| // Actual logic at minimal nesting
00516| ```
00517| 
00518| ### Variable Scope Minimization
00519| 
00520| **Guideline:** Declare variables in the smallest scope possible.
00521| 
00522| ✅ **Good:**
00523| ```c
00524| EFI_STATUS MyFunction(VOID)
00525| {
00526|   EFI_STATUS  Status;
00527|   
00528|   // ... some code ...
00529|   
00530|   {
00531|     UINT32  TempValue;  // Scoped to this block only
00532|     TempValue = CalculateSomething();
00533|     Status = ProcessValue(TempValue);
00534|   }
00535|   
00536|   return Status;
00537| }
00538| ```
00539| 
00540| ---
00541| 
00542| ## 8. Error Handling Patterns
00543| 
00544| ### Consistent Error Handling
00545| 
00546| **Guideline:** Use consistent error handling patterns throughout a module.
00547| 
00548| ✅ **Consistent Pattern:**
00549| ```c
00550| Status = Step1();
00551| if (EFI_ERROR(Status)) {
00552|   DEBUG((DEBUG_ERROR, "Step1 failed: %r\n", Status));
00553|   goto Exit;
00554| }
00555| 
00556| Status = Step2();
00557| if (EFI_ERROR(Status)) {
00558|   DEBUG((DEBUG_ERROR, "Step2 failed: %r\n", Status));
00559|   goto Exit;
00560| }
00561| 
00562| Exit:
00563|   // Cleanup
00564|   return Status;
00565| ```
00566| 
00567| ### Resource Cleanup
00568| 
00569| **Guideline:** Always clean up resources in error paths.
00570| 
00571| ✅ **Good - Cleanup on Error:**
00572| ```c
00573| Buffer = AllocatePool(Size);
00574| if (Buffer == NULL) {
00575|   return EFI_OUT_OF_RESOURCES;
00576| }
00577| 
00578| Status = ProcessBuffer(Buffer);
00579| if (EFI_ERROR(Status)) {
00580|   FreePool(Buffer);  // Cleanup before return
00581|   return Status;
00582| }
00583| 
00584| FreePool(Buffer);
00585| return EFI_SUCCESS;
00586| ```
00587| 
00588| ### Error Message Clarity
00589| 
00590| **Guideline:** Debug messages should clearly identify the error source.
00591| 
00592| ✅ **Good:**
00593| ```c
00594| DEBUG((DEBUG_ERROR, "[NVRAM] Failed to allocate buffer (Size=%d): %r\n", BufferSize, Status));
00595| ```
00596| 
00597| ⚠️ **Needs Improvement:**
00598| ```c
00599| DEBUG((DEBUG_ERROR, "Error: %r\n", Status));  // INFO: Add context (module, operation)
00600| ```
00601| 
00602| ---
00603| 
00604| ## 9. Performance Considerations
00605| 
00606| ### Unnecessary Memory Allocations
00607| 
00608| **Guideline:** Avoid allocations in performance-critical paths.
00609| 
00610| ⚠️ **Optimization Opportunity (INFO):**
00611| ```c
00612| for (Index = 0; Index < Count; Index++) {
00613|   Buffer = AllocatePool(Size);  // INFO: Consider allocating once before loop
00614|   ProcessBuffer(Buffer);
00615|   FreePool(Buffer);
00616| }
00617| ```
00618| 
00619| ✅ **Better:**
00620| ```c
00621| Buffer = AllocatePool(Size);
00622| for (Index = 0; Index < Count; Index++) {
00623|   ProcessBuffer(Buffer);
00624| }
00625| FreePool(Buffer);
00626| ```
00627| 
00628| ### Repeated Calculations
00629| 
00630| **Guideline:** Cache calculated values instead of recalculating.
00631| 
00632| ⚠️ **Repeated Calculation (INFO):**
00633| ```c
00634| for (Index = 0; Index < Count; Index++) {
00635|   Process(Data[Index] * sizeof(ELEMENT_TYPE));  // INFO: Calculate once
00636| }
00637| ```
00638| 
00639| ✅ **Better:**
00640| ```c
00641| ElementSize = sizeof(ELEMENT_TYPE);
00642| for (Index = 0; Index < Count; Index++) {
00643|   Process(Data[Index] * ElementSize);
00644| }
00645| ```
00646| 
00647| ### String Operations in Loops
00648| 
00649| **Guideline:** Minimize string operations in tight loops.
00650| 
00651| ⚠️ **Performance Note (INFO):**
00652| ```c
00653| for (Index = 0; Index < Count; Index++) {
00654|   StrCpy(Buffer, L"Prefix");  // INFO: Move string ops outside loop if possible
00655|   StrCat(Buffer, Names[Index]);
00656|   ProcessString(Buffer);
00657| }
00658| ```
00659| 
00660| ---
00661| 
00662| ## 10. Maintainability
00663| 
00664| ### Code Duplication
00665| 
00666| **Guideline:** Extract common code into reusable functions.
00667| 
00668| ⚠️ **Duplication Detected (INFO):**
00669| ```c
00670| // In Function1:
00671| Status = ValidateInput(Input);
00672| if (EFI_ERROR(Status)) {
00673|   DEBUG((DEBUG_ERROR, "Invalid input: %r\n", Status));
00674|   return Status;
00675| }
00676| 
00677| // In Function2:
00678| Status = ValidateInput(Input);  // INFO: Extract to helper function
00679| if (EFI_ERROR(Status)) {
00680|   DEBUG((DEBUG_ERROR, "Invalid input: %r\n", Status));
00681|   return Status;
00682| }
00683| ```
00684| 
00685| ### Magic Numbers
00686| 
00687| **Guideline:** Replace magic numbers with named constants.
00688| 
00689| ⚠️ **Magic Number (INFO):**
00690| ```c
00691| if (Size > 0x10000) {  // INFO: Use named constant
00692|   return EFI_BUFFER_TOO_SMALL;
00693| }
00694| ```
00695| 
00696| ✅ **Better:**
00697| ```c
00698| #define MAX_BUFFER_SIZE  0x10000  // 64 KB
00699| 
00700| if (Size > MAX_BUFFER_SIZE) {
00701|   return EFI_BUFFER_TOO_SMALL;
00702| }
00703| ```
00704| 
00705| ### Complex Boolean Expressions
00706| 
00707| **Guideline:** Simplify complex conditions for clarity.
00708| 
00709| ⚠️ **Complex Expression (INFO):**
00710| ```c
00711| if ((Flags & FLAG_A) && (Flags & FLAG_B) && !(Flags & FLAG_C) && 
00712|     (Status == EFI_SUCCESS || Status == EFI_WARN_RESET_REQUIRED)) {
00713|   // INFO: Consider extracting to named boolean or helper function
00714| }
00715| ```
00716| 
00717| ✅ **Better:**
00718| ```c
00719| BOOLEAN IsValidState(UINT32 Flags, EFI_STATUS Status)
00720| {
00721|   if (!(Flags & FLAG_A) || !(Flags & FLAG_B)) return FALSE;
00722|   if (Flags & FLAG_C) return FALSE;
00723|   if (Status != EFI_SUCCESS && Status != EFI_WARN_RESET_REQUIRED) return FALSE;
00724|   return TRUE;
00725| }
00726| 
00727| if (IsValidState(Flags, Status)) {
00728|   // Clear logic
00729| }
00730| ```
00731| 
00732| ---
00733| 
00734| ## 11. Firmware-Specific Patterns
00735| 
00736| ### Boot Path Optimization
00737| 
00738| **Guideline:** Optimize boot path for minimal delay.
00739| 
00740| **Observations (INFO):**
00741| - Defer non-critical initialization to later boot phases
00742| - Minimize PEI phase memory allocations
00743| - Avoid unnecessary protocol installs in early boot
00744| - Cache frequently accessed data
00745| 
00746| **Example:**
00747| ```c
00748| // INFO: Consider deferring feature init to BDS phase
00749| if (IsBootPhase(DXE_CORE)) {
00750|   // Defer non-critical features
00751|   RegisterProtocolNotify(&gEfiBdsArchProtocolGuid, DeferredInit);
00752| } else {
00753|   // Critical path only
00754|   InitializeCriticalHardware();
00755| }
00756| ```
00757| 
00758| ### Memory Footprint
00759| 
00760| **Guideline:** Minimize memory usage, especially in PEI.
00761| 
00762| **Observations (INFO):**
00763| - Large static arrays suggest pool allocation
00764| - Temporary buffers should be freed promptly
00765| - Consider pool vs page allocation based on size
00766| - PEI HOBs should be minimal
00767| 
00768| **Example:**
00769| ```c
00770| // INFO: Large array increases footprint
00771| STATIC UINT8 mLargeBuffer[64 * 1024];  // 64KB static allocation
00772| 
00773| // Consider:
00774| UINT8  *Buffer = AllocatePool(RequiredSize);  // Allocate only what's needed
00775| ```
00776| 
00777| ### S3 Resume Path Safety
00778| 
00779| **Guideline:** Ensure S3 resume path uses only S3-safe resources.
00780| 
00781| **Observations (INFO):**
00782| - No runtime variable access in S3 path (use boot script)
00783| - No protocol dependencies in S3 resume
00784| - Hardware state must be restored from saved boot script
00785| - SMM handlers must be S3-aware
00786| 
00787| **Example:**
00788| ```c
00789| // INFO: Variable access not S3-safe
00790| if (IsS3Resume()) {
00791|   // Use boot script data, not fresh variable reads
00792|   RestoreFromBootScript();
00793| } else {
00794|   GetVariable(L"Config", ...);
00795| }
00796| ```
00797| 
00798| ---
00799| 
00800| ## 12. Additional Style Recommendations
00801| 
00802| ### Whitespace
00803| 
00804| **Guideline:** Consistent whitespace improves readability.
00805| 
00806| - Blank line between function definitions
00807| - Blank line between logical code blocks
00808| - Space after keywords: `if (`, `while (`, `for (`
00809| - No trailing whitespace
00810| 
00811| ### Bracket Style
00812| 
00813| **Guideline:** Follow consistent bracket placement.
00814| 
00815| ✅ **Preferred (UEFI Style):**
00816| ```c
00817| if (Condition)
00818| {
00819|   // Code
00820| }
00821| ```
00822| 
00823| **Acceptable:**
00824| ```c
00825| if (Condition) {
00826|   // Code
00827| }
00828| ```
00829| 
00830| **Choose one and be consistent within a file.**
00831| 
00832| ### Typedef vs Struct
00833| 
00834| **Guideline:** Use typedefs for complex structures.
00835| 
00836| ✅ **Good:**
00837| ```c
00838| typedef struct {
00839|   UINT32  Field1;
00840|   UINT16  Field2;
00841| } MY_STRUCTURE;
00842| 
00843| MY_STRUCTURE  MyVar;  // Clean usage
00844| ```
00845| 
00846| ---
00847| 
00848| ## Validation Checklist (All INFO Severity)
00849| 
00850| When reviewing for Firmware Coding Standards:
00851| 
00852| ☐ **Comments**
00853|   - [ ] Function headers complete with @param/@retval
00854|   - [ ] Complex logic has explanatory comments
00855|   - [ ] TODO/FIXME properly marked
00856| 
00857| ☐ **File Structure**
00858|   - [ ] C files follow correct order (header → includes → constants → variables → functions)
00859|   - [ ] Function declarations before definitions
00860|   - [ ] No mixed ordering of sections
00861|   - [ ] Related declarations grouped together
00862| 
00863| ☐ **Header Files**
00864|   - [ ] No code implementations in headers (declarations only)
00865|   - [ ] Include guards present and correctly named
00866|   - [ ] Headers are self-contained
00867|   - [ ] Unnecessary includes minimized
00868| 
00869| ☐ **Line Formatting**
00870|   - [ ] One statement per line
00871|   - [ ] Consistent operator spacing
00872|   - [ ] One variable per line (exceptions for related simple vars)
00873|   - [ ] No trailing whitespace
00874| 
00875| ☐ **Naming Quality**
00876|   - [ ] Names are meaningful and descriptive
00877|   - [ ] No ambiguous names (Size1, Var2, etc.)
00878|   - [ ] No case-only name differences
00879|   - [ ] Abbreviations are clear and consistent
00880| 
00881| ☐ **Porting Markers**
00882|   - [ ] AMI modifications to third-party code wrapped with `//*** AMI PORTING BEGIN/END ***`
00883|   - [ ] Markers include brief explanation
00884|   - [ ] Only changed code inside markers
00885|   - [ ] Correct syntax used
00886| 
00887| ☐ **Readability**
00888|   - [ ] Lines under 120 characters (where reasonable)
00889|   - [ ] Functions under 200 lines
00890|   - [ ] Nesting depth under 5 levels
00891|   - [ ] Variables scoped minimally
00892| 
00893| ☐ **Error Handling**
00894|   - [ ] Consistent error handling pattern
00895|   - [ ] All error paths clean up resources
00896|   - [ ] Error messages are clear and specific
00897| 
00898| ☐ **Performance**
00899|   - [ ] No unnecessary allocations in loops
00900|   - [ ] Calculations cached when repeated
00901|   - [ ] String operations minimized in tight loops
00902| 
00903| ☐ **Maintainability**
00904|   - [ ] No significant code duplication
00905|   - [ ] Magic numbers replaced with constants
00906|   - [ ] Complex expressions simplified
00907| 
00908| ☐ **Firmware-Specific**
00909|   - [ ] Boot path optimized (deferred initialization)
00910|   - [ ] Memory footprint considered
00911|   - [ ] S3 resume path uses safe resources only
00912| 
00913| ---
00914| 
00915| ## Reference
00916| 
00917| **Document:** AMI Aptio 5.x Firmware Coding Standards  
00918| **Workspace Instructions:** `.github/copilot-instructions.md`
00919| 
00920| **Related Standards:**
00921| - Module Developer's Guide (MANDATORY) - `.github/skills/ami-module-developer-standards/`
00922| - UEFI/PI/EDK2 specs - covered in main review stages
00923| 
00924| This document covers **firmware-specific style recommendations only** - all findings are informational and do not impact review verdict.
===== END: .github\skills\ami-firmware-coding-standards\Firmware_Coding_Standards.md =====

00001| ---
00002| name: ami-firmware-coding-standards
00003| description: 'AMI Aptio 5.x Firmware Coding Standards validation (INFORMATIONAL). Use when checking firmware-specific coding standards compliance after UEFI code review and Module Developer Guide validation.'
00004| user-invocable: false
00005| disable-model-invocation: false

00007| 
00008| # AMI Firmware Coding Standards Validation (INFORMATIONAL)
00009| 
00010| ## Purpose
00011| Validate reviewed code against **informational** AMI Aptio 5.x Firmware Coding Standards that provide additional style guidance beyond Module Developer's Guide requirements.
00012| 
00013| **Compliance Level:** INFORMATIONAL - Violations do NOT impact the final review verdict. Findings are tracked for post-merge cleanup.
00014| 
00015| ## When to Use
00016| - **After Module Developer's Guide validation** (Step 3.8 complete)
00017| - To check firmware-specific style and best practices
00018| - Only on files that were already reviewed and passed Module Developer Guide validation
00019| 
00020| ## Scope
00021| This skill checks **firmware-specific style recommendations** only. UEFI/PI/EDK2 spec compliance and Module Developer Guide mandatory standards are already covered.
00022| 
00023| ## Standards Reference
00024| See [Firmware Coding Standards](./Firmware_Coding_Standards.md) for complete firmware-specific style recommendations.
00025| 
00026| ## Validation Procedure
00027| 
00028| ### Step 1: Identify Files in Scope
00029| Review only files that were checked in Module Developer Guide validation (Step 3.8). Do not review files that were skipped or excluded.
00030| 
00031| ### Step 2: Classify File Ownership (AMI vs Third-Party)
00032| 
00033| Use the same classification logic as Module Developer Guide:
00034| 
00035| **Classification Rules:**
00036| 
00037| | Condition | Classification | Validation Scope |
00038| |-----------|----------------|------------------|
00039| | File contains AMI copyright banner (`Copyright (c) 1985-20XX, AMI`) | ✅ **AMI-owned** | Entire file |
00040| | File contains third-party copyright AND contains AMI override markers | ⚙️ **Third-party with AMI overrides** | AMI override sections only |
00041| | File contains NO copyright header | ✅ **AMI-owned** (default assumption) | Entire file |
00042| | File contains only third-party copyright, NO AMI override markers | ❌ **Third-party only** | Skip validation |
00043| 
00044| **AMI Override Marker Patterns (case-insensitive):**
00045| 
00046| | Start Marker | End Marker |
00047| |--------------|------------|
00048| | `// AMI OVERRIDE START` | `// AMI OVERRIDE END` |
00049| | `//APTIOV_SERVER_OVERRIDE_START:` | `//APTIOV_SERVER_OVERRIDE_END:` |
00050| | `//APTIOV_SERVER_OVERRIDE_RC_START:` | `//APTIOV_SERVER_OVERRIDE_RC_END:` |
00051| 
00052| ### Step 3: Extract AMI Code Sections
00053| 
00054| Same extraction logic as Module Developer Guide validation (entire file vs override sections only).
00055| 
00056| ### Step 4: Check Firmware Coding Standards
00057| 
00058| For each AMI code section, verify firmware-specific style recommendations:
00059| 
00060| 1. **Comment Style** (INFORMATIONAL)
00061|    - Function header comments completeness
00062|    - Code comment clarity and usefulness
00063|    - TODO/FIXME/HACK marker usage
00064| 
00065| 2. **Code Readability** (INFORMATIONAL)
00066|    - Line length recommendations (prefer < 120 characters)
00067|    - Function length guidelines (prefer < 200 lines)
00068|    - Nesting depth (prefer < 5 levels)
00069|    - Variable scope minimization
00070| 
00071| 3. **Error Handling Patterns** (INFORMATIONAL)
00072|    - Consistent error handling style
00073|    - Resource cleanup in error paths
00074|    - Error message clarity
00075| 
00076| 4. **Performance Considerations** (INFORMATIONAL)
00077|    - Unnecessary memory allocations
00078|    - Repeated calculations that could be cached
00079|    - String operations in loops
00080| 
00081| 5. **Maintainability** (INFORMATIONAL)
00082|    - Code duplication detection
00083|    - Magic number usage (prefer named constants)
00084|    - Complex boolean expressions (suggest simplification)
00085| 
00086| 6. **Firmware-Specific Patterns** (INFORMATIONAL)
00087|    - Boot path optimization hints
00088|    - Memory footprint considerations
00089|    - S3 resume path safety
00090| 
00091| ### Step 5: Report Findings
00092| 
00093| Append findings to the review report under a new section:
00094| 
00095| ```markdown
00096| ---
00097| 
00098| ## AMI Firmware Coding Standards (INFORMATIONAL)
00099| 
00100| Files validated against AMI Aptio 5.x Firmware Coding Standards style recommendations.
00101| 
00102| ### Summary
00103| - Files reviewed for style: X
00104| - Informational observations: Y
00105| 
00106| ### Findings
00107| 
00108| **[AmiModulePkg/Nvram/NvramDxe.c]** (AMI-owned)
00109| - Comment Style: Function header missing parameter description
00110|   - Location: Line 147
00111|   - Severity: INFO (style recommendation)
00112|   - Suggestion: Add `@param` Doxygen tags for parameters
00113| - Code Readability: Function exceeds recommended length (215 lines)
00114|   - Location: Lines 200-415
00115|   - Severity: INFO (maintainability)
00116|   - Suggestion: Consider refactoring into smaller functions
00117| 
00118| **[Intel/ClientOneSiliconPkg/Cpu/PeiCpuPolicyLib.c]** (Third-party with AMI overrides)
00119| - [Third-party file — reviewing AMI override sections only: lines 245-260]
00120| - Performance: Repeated calculation in loop
00121|   - Location: Line 252 (inside `//APTIOV_SERVER_OVERRIDE_START:` block)
00122|   - Severity: INFO (optimization opportunity)
00123|   - Suggestion: Calculate `BufferSize = sizeof(BUFFER) * Count` once before loop
00124| 
00125| ### Firmware Coding Standards Verdict
00126| ✅ **STYLE COMPLIANT** / ℹ️ **STYLE OBSERVATIONS** (informational only)
00127| 
00128| > **Note:** Firmware Coding Standards violations are **INFORMATIONAL ONLY** and do not affect the main review verdict (APPROVE/CONDITIONAL/BLOCK). Findings are tracked for post-merge cleanup or next revision.
00129| ```
00130| 
00131| **Reporting Guidelines:**
00132| 
00133| 1. **All findings are INFO severity** - no MAJOR/MINOR/CRITICAL
00134| 2. **Use "Suggestion:" instead of "Fix:"** - these are recommendations, not requirements
00135| 3. **Group by file classification** (same as Module Developer Guide)
00136| 4. **For AMI override sections:** Include line range notation
00137| 5. **If no findings:** Write: `> All reviewed files follow firmware coding style recommendations.`
00138| 
00139| ### Step 6: Final Notes
00140| - **Does NOT impact** the main review verdict (APPROVE/CONDITIONAL/BLOCK)
00141| - Firmware Coding Standards violations are **INFORMATIONAL ONLY**
00142| - Findings are suggestions for code quality improvement
00143| - **AMI code only:** Only AMI-owned files and AMI override sections validated
00144| - **Third-party code skipped:** Pure third-party code not validated
00145| - **Post-merge tracking:** All findings go to post-merge improvement backlog
00146| 
00147| ## Severity Levels
00148| 
00149| All Firmware Coding Standards findings use **INFO** severity only:
00150| 
00151| | Category | Severity | Examples |
00152| |----------|----------|----------|
00153| | Comment Style | INFO | Missing parameter docs, unclear comments |
00154| | Code Readability | INFO | Long functions, deep nesting, long lines |
00155| | Error Handling | INFO | Inconsistent error handling patterns |
00156| | Performance | INFO | Optimization opportunities, repeated calculations |
00157| | Maintainability | INFO | Code duplication, magic numbers, complex expressions |
00158| | Firmware-Specific | INFO | Boot path optimization, memory footprint hints |
00159| 
00160| **No findings impact verdict or score.**
00161| 
00162| ## Progressive Loading
00163| The skill loads lightweight validation logic first, then references [Firmware_Coding_Standards.md](./Firmware_Coding_Standards.md) only when detailed guideline text is needed for citation.

===== FILE: .github\skills\ami-module-developer-standards\Module_Developer_Standards.md  (603 lines) =====
00001| # AMI Aptio 5.x Module Developer's Guide Standards (MANDATORY)
00002| 
00003| **Source:** AMI Aptio 5.x Module Developer's Guide  
00004| **Compliance Level:** MANDATORY - Violations impact review verdict  
00005| **Scope:** AMI-specific organization standards extending UEFI 2.10+, PI 1.8+, EDK II C Coding Standard 2.2+
00006| 
00007| ---
00008| 
00009| ## Violation Severity Levels
00010| 
00011| Module Developer Guide violations are classified by severity and impact the final review verdict:
00012| 
00013| | Severity | Impact on Verdict | Examples |
00014| |----------|-------------------|----------|
00015| | **MANDATORY-MAJOR** | Added to main review MAJOR count; may trigger CONDITIONAL APPROVE or BLOCK | Raw C types (`int`, `char`, `void`), missing AMI OVERRIDE markers in Intel/EDK2 files, wrong customization layer |
00016| | **MANDATORY-MINOR** | Added to main review MINOR count; impacts quality score | Copyright year outdated, function naming case incorrect, section order wrong, include order violations |
00017| | **INFO** | Informational only; no verdict impact | Suggestions for improvement, optional enhancements |
00018| 
00019| **Verdict Impact Rules:**
00020| - MANDATORY-MAJOR findings are treated as MAJOR findings in the main review
00021| - MANDATORY-MINOR findings are treated as MINOR findings in the main review
00022| - Verdict is recalculated after Module Developer Guide validation
00023| - If new findings push verdict from APPROVE → CONDITIONAL or BLOCK, `Result.txt` is updated
00024| 
00025| ---
00026| 
00027| ## Validation Scope: AMI Code Only (MANDATORY Compliance)
00028| 
00029| These standards are **MANDATORY** for AMI-owned code. Violations impact the final review verdict.
00030| 
00031| ### File Classification
00032| 
00033| | File Type | How to Identify | Validation Scope |
00034| |-----------|----------------|------------------|
00035| | **AMI-owned** | Contains `Copyright (c) 1985-YYYY, AMI` | **Entire file** |
00036| | **Third-party with AMI overrides** | Third-party copyright + AMI override markers | **AMI override sections only** |
00037| | **No copyright** | No copyright header found | **Entire file** (default to AMI) |
00038| | **Third-party only** | Third-party copyright, no AMI markers | **Skip validation** |
00039| 
00040| ### AMI Override Markers in Third-Party Files
00041| 
00042| When AMI modifies third-party files (Intel, EDK2, etc.), changes are wrapped in override markers. **Only code between these markers is validated** against AMI organization standards.
00043| 
00044| **Standard Marker Patterns:**
00045| 
00046| | Start Marker | End Marker | Usage |
00047| |--------------|------------|-------|
00048| | `// AMI OVERRIDE START` | `// AMI OVERRIDE END` | General EDK2/Intel file modifications |
00049| | `//APTIOV_SERVER_OVERRIDE_START:` | `//APTIOV_SERVER_OVERRIDE_END:` | Server platform overrides |
00050| | `//APTIOV_SERVER_OVERRIDE_RC_START:` | `//APTIOV_SERVER_OVERRIDE_RC_END:` | Server platform RC overrides |
00051| 
00052| **Pattern Recognition (case-insensitive):**
00053| ```regex
00054| Start: ^\s*//\s*(AMI|APTIOV)[A-Z_]*OVERRIDE[A-Z_]*START
00055| End:   ^\s*//\s*(AMI|APTIOV)[A-Z_]*OVERRIDE[A-Z_]*END
00056| ```
00057| 
00058| **Example: Third-Party File with AMI Overrides**
00059| 
00060| ```c
00061| /** @file
00062|   Copyright (c) 2020 - 2024, Intel Corporation. All rights reserved.
00063|   SPDX-License-Identifier: BSD-2-Clause-Patent
00064| **/
00065| 
00066| #include <Uefi.h>
00067| 
00068| // ⬇️ Third-party code - NOT validated against AMI standards
00069| void IntelFunction() {
00070|   int bufferSize = 100;  // Raw C types OK in Intel code
00071| }
00072| 
00073| // ⬇️ AMI override section - VALIDATED against AMI standards
00074| // AMI OVERRIDE START — Add custom validation
00075| BOOLEAN gAmiValidationEnabled = TRUE;  // Must use UEFI types + 'g' prefix
00076| 
00077| EFI_STATUS AmiCustomValidation(VOID) {  // Must use PascalCase
00078|   return EFI_SUCCESS;
00079| }
00080| // AMI OVERRIDE END
00081| // ⬆️ End of AMI code
00082| 
00083| // ⬇️ Third-party code resumes - NOT validated
00084| void AnotherIntelFunction() {
00085|   // Intel code continues
00086| }
00087| ```
00088| 
00089| **In this example:**
00090| - Lines outside override markers: **Not validated** (Intel code)
00091| - Lines between `// AMI OVERRIDE START` and `// AMI OVERRIDE END`: **Validated** against all applicable AMI standards (naming, types, patterns)
00092| - File header (copyright): **Not validated** (remains Intel copyright)
00093| 
00094| ---
00095| 
00096| ## 1. File Header Format
00097| 
00098| **Applies to:** AMI-owned files only. Third-party files with AMI overrides keep their original header.
00099| 
00100| Every AMI source file (.c, .h) must use the AMI copyright banner followed by a Doxygen `@file` block:
00101| 
00102| ```c
00103| //***********************************************************************
00104| //*                                                                     *
00105| //*                 Copyright (c) 1985-20XX, AMI.                       *
00106| //*                                                                     *
00107| //*      All rights reserved. Subject to AMI licensing agreement.       *
00108| //*                                                                     *
00109| //***********************************************************************
00110| 
00111| /** @file ModuleName.c
00112|     Brief description of the file's purpose
00113| **/
00114| ```
00115| 
00116| ### Requirements
00117| - Copyright year must include current year (20XX updated annually) - **MANDATORY-MINOR** if outdated
00118| - `@file` block must have file name matching actual file name - **MANDATORY-MINOR** if missing or incorrect
00119| - Brief description must be present and meaningful (not "Module file") - **MANDATORY-MINOR** if generic
00120| 
00121| ---
00122| 
00123| ## 2. Source File Organization
00124| 
00125| Files are organized with `//------` separator lines into sections in this **strict order**:
00126| 
00127| ### Section Order (Mandatory)
00128| 
00129| 1. **Include(s)**
00130| 2. **Constant, Macro and Type Definition(s)**
00131|    - Sub-groups: Constant, Macro, Type, Function Prototype
00132| 3. **Variable and External Declaration(s)**
00133|    - Sub-groups: Variable, GUID, Protocol/PPI, External, Function definitions
00134| 
00135| ### Example Structure
00136| 
00137| ```c
00138| //***********************************************************************
00139| // Copyright & @file block
00140| //***********************************************************************
00141| 
00142| //---------------------------------------------------------------------------
00143| // Include(s)
00144| //---------------------------------------------------------------------------
00145| #include <Uefi.h>
00146| #include <Token.h>
00147| #include <AmiDxeLib.h>
00148| 
00149| //---------------------------------------------------------------------------
00150| // Constant, Macro and Type Definition(s)
00151| //---------------------------------------------------------------------------
00152| 
00153| // Constant Definition(s)
00154| #define MAX_BUFFER_SIZE  256
00155| 
00156| // Macro Definition(s)
00157| #define GET_ELEMENT(x)  ((x) & 0xFF)
00158| 
00159| // Type Definition(s)
00160| typedef struct {
00161|   UINT32  Field1;
00162|   UINT16  Field2;
00163| } MY_STRUCTURE;
00164| 
00165| // Function Prototype(s)
00166| EFI_STATUS InitializeModule(VOID);
00167| 
00168| //---------------------------------------------------------------------------
00169| // Variable and External Declaration(s)
00170| //---------------------------------------------------------------------------
00171| 
00172| // Variable Declaration(s)
00173| STATIC UINT8  mLocalBuffer[MAX_BUFFER_SIZE];
00174| 
00175| // GUID Definition(s)
00176| EFI_GUID gMyProtocolGuid = MY_PROTOCOL_GUID;
00177| 
00178| // Protocol/PPI Declaration(s)
00179| MY_PROTOCOL  *gMyProtocol = NULL;
00180| 
00181| // External Declaration(s)
00182| extern UINT32  gExternalValue;
00183| 
00184| // Function Definition(s)
00185| EFI_STATUS
00186| InitializeModule (
00187|   VOID
00188|   )
00189| {
00190|   // Implementation
00191| }
00192| ```
00193| 
00194| ---
00195| 
00196| ## 3. Naming Conventions
00197| 
00198| ### Raw C Types (MANDATORY-MAJOR)
00199| 
00200| **NEVER use raw C types** — they cause ABI incompatibility, platform-dependent sizes, and runtime failures.
00201| 
00202| | ❌ Prohibited (Raw C) | ✅ Required (UEFI) | Reason |
00203| |---------------------|-------------------|---------|
00204| | `int` | `UINT32` or `INT32` | Size varies (16/32 bits) |
00205| | `unsigned` | `UINT32` or `UINTN` | Platform-dependent |
00206| | `char` | `CHAR8` | Sign varies by compiler |
00207| | `void` | `VOID` | UEFI convention |
00208| | `long` | `UINT64` or `INT64` | Size varies (32/64 bits) |
00209| | `short` | `UINT16` or `INT16` | Not portable |
00210| 
00211| **Examples of MANDATORY-MAJOR violations:**
00212| ```c
00213| // ❌ WRONG - Raw C types
00214| int bufferSize = 100;
00215| char name[64];
00216| unsigned index;
00217| void *ptr;
00218| extern int VfrBin[];
00219| 
00220| // ✅ CORRECT - UEFI types
00221| UINT32 BufferSize = 100;
00222| CHAR8 Name[64];
00223| UINTN Index;
00224| VOID *Ptr;
00225| extern UINT8 VfrBin[];
00226| ```
00227| 
00228| **Severity:** **MANDATORY-MAJOR** — Treated as MAJOR finding in main review; may trigger CONDITIONAL APPROVE or BLOCK verdict.
00229| 
00230| ---
00231| 
00232| ### Functions
00233| 
00234| | Pattern | Convention | Example |
00235| |---------|-----------|---------|
00236| | General functions | PascalCase | `InitializeDriver`, `ProcessBuffer` |
00237| | Entry points | `<Module>Init` or `<Module>EntryPoint` | `CrbDxeInit`, `SmbiosPeiInit` |
00238| | Static/local functions | PascalCase (prefix optional) | `ValidateInput`, `LocalHelper` |
00239| 
00240| ### Variables
00241| 
00242| | Pattern | Convention | Example |
00243| |---------|-----------|---------|
00244| | Global variables | `g` prefix + PascalCase | `gBootScript`, `gAmiCrbInfoPpi` |
00245| | Registration variables | `g<Name>Reg` | `gAmiConOutStartedProtocolReg` |
00246| | Module-scope static | `m` prefix + PascalCase | `mLocalBuffer`, `mInitialized` |
00247| | Local variables | camelCase or PascalCase | `BufferSize`, `status` |
00248| 
00249| ### Constants and Macros
00250| 
00251| - ALL_CAPS with underscores: `MAX_BUFFER_SIZE`, `ENABLE_DEBUG_MODE`
00252| - Token names in SDL: ALL_CAPS with underscores: `DEBUG_MODE`, `CRB_BOARD`
00253| 
00254| ### Types
00255| 
00256| - UEFI types: `EFI_STATUS`, `VOID`, `UINT8`, `UINT16`, `UINT32`, `UINT64`, `UINTN`, `BOOLEAN`, `EFI_HANDLE`
00257| - Custom types: PascalCase with all-caps suffix: `MY_PROTOCOL`, `SETUP_DATA`, `GPIO_CONFIG`
00258| - Pointer types: use UEFI style: `EFI_HANDLE *Handle` (not `EFI_HANDLE* Handle`)
00259| 
00260| ---
00261| 
00262| ## 4. Include Order
00263| 
00264| Includes must follow this order:
00265| 
00266| ```c
00267| #include <Uefi.h>       // or <Pei.h> for PEI modules - FIRST
00268| #include <Token.h>       // auto-generated SDL token definitions
00269| #include <AmiDxeLib.h>   // AMI DXE library (or <AmiPeiLib.h> for PEI)
00270| #include <Library/PcdLib.h>       // EDK2 standard libraries
00271| #include <Library/BaseMemoryLib.h>
00272| #include <Protocol/SomeProtocol.h> // Protocol / PPI headers
00273| #include <SetupVariable.h>         // Platform-specific headers
00274| ```
00275| 
00276| ### Rules
00277| 1. **Phase header first:** `<Uefi.h>` or `<Pei.h>` or `<Base.h>`
00278| 2. **AMI headers next:** `<Token.h>`, `<AmiDxeLib.h>` / `<AmiPeiLib.h>`
00279| 3. **EDK2 libraries:** `<Library/...>`
00280| 4. **Protocol/PPI/GUID headers:** `<Protocol/...>`, `<Ppi/...>`, `<Guid/...>`
00281| 5. **Platform headers last:** Setup, board-specific
00282| 
00283| ---
00284| 
00285| ## 5. INF File Structure
00286| 
00287| INF file sections must appear in this order:
00288| 
00289| ```ini
00290| [Defines]
00291|   INF_VERSION                    = 0x00010005
00292|   BASE_NAME                      = ModuleName
00293|   FILE_GUID                      = XXXXXXXX-XXXX-XXXX-XXXX-XXXXXXXXXXXX
00294|   MODULE_TYPE                    = DXE_DRIVER
00295|   VERSION_STRING                 = 1.0
00296|   ENTRY_POINT                    = ModuleInit
00297| 
00298| [Sources]
00299|   ModuleFile.c
00300|   ModuleHeader.h
00301| 
00302| [Packages]
00303|   MdePkg/MdePkg.dec              # MUST be first
00304|   AmiModulePkg/AmiModulePkg.dec
00305|   AmiChipsetModulePkg/AmiChipsetModulePkg.dec
00306| 
00307| [LibraryClasses]
00308|   UefiDriverEntryPoint           # Entry point library
00309|   UefiBootServicesTableLib
00310|   BaseMemoryLib
00311| 
00312| [Protocols]
00313|   gEfiSomeProtocolGuid           # PRODUCED
00314|   gEfiOtherProtocolGuid          # CONSUMED
00315| 
00316| [Guids]
00317|   gEfiEventGroupGuid
00318| 
00319| [Pcd]
00320|   gEfiMdePkgTokenSpaceGuid.PcdSomeValue
00321| 
00322| [Depex]
00323|   gEfiSomeProtocolGuid AND gEfiOtherProtocolGuid
00324| ```
00325| 
00326| ### Requirements
00327| - `MdePkg/MdePkg.dec` must be **first** in `[Packages]` - **MANDATORY-MINOR** if not first
00328| - Entry-point library must be **first** in `[LibraryClasses]` - **MANDATORY-MINOR** if not first
00329| - Section order must match above - **MANDATORY-MINOR** if out of order
00330| - Comment PRODUCED vs CONSUMED for protocols/PPIs when not obvious
00331| 
00332| ---
00333| 
00334| ## 6. AMI Override Marker Policy
00335| 
00336| `// AMI OVERRIDE START/END` markers are **required only** when modifying EDK2 or Intel-owned files. They are **never** used in AMI-owned packages.
00337| 
00338| ### When Markers are Required
00339| 
00340| | Package path | Markers required? | Violation if missing |
00341| |---|---|---|
00342| | `Intel/` (any Intel package) | ✅ **YES** | **MANDATORY-MAJOR** |
00343| | `MdePkg/`, `MdeModulePkg/`, `SecurityPkg/`, `CryptoPkg/`, `UefiCpuPkg/` | ✅ **YES** | **MANDATORY-MAJOR** |
00344| | `Ami*Pkg/`, `CrbPkg/`, `AmiCrbPkg/`, vendor packages | ❌ **NO** | N/A |
00345| 
00346| ### Syntax (EDK2 / Intel files only)
00347| 
00348| ```c
00349| // AMI OVERRIDE START — <brief description> (<YYYY-MM-DD>)
00350| ... changed code ...
00351| // AMI OVERRIDE END
00352| ```
00353| 
00354| **Example:**
00355| ```c
00356| // AMI OVERRIDE START — Add SMRAM boundary validation (2026-05-15)
00357| if (Address < mSmramBase || Address > mSmramEnd) {
00358|   return EFI_ACCESS_DENIED;
00359| }
00360| // AMI OVERRIDE END
00361| ```
00362| 
00363| ---
00364| 
00365| ## 7. SDL Token Patterns
00366| 
00367| ### Token Declaration
00368| 
00369| ```sdl
00370| TOKEN
00371|     Name  = "MY_FEATURE_ENABLE"
00372|     Value  = "1"
00373|     Help  = "Enable my feature support"
00374|     TokenType = Boolean
00375|     TargetMAK = Yes
00376|     TargetH = Yes
00377| End
00378| ```
00379| 
00380| ### Token Override (Higher Layer)
00381| 
00382| ```sdl
00383| TOKEN
00384|     Name  = "MY_FEATURE_ENABLE"
00385|     Value  = "0"
00386|     TokenType = Boolean
00387|     TargetMAK = Yes
00388|     TargetH = Yes
00389| End
00390| ```
00391| 
00392| ### PCD Mapping
00393| 
00394| ```sdl
00395| PcdMapping
00396|     Name  = "PcdMyValue"
00397|     GuidSpace  = "gMyTokenSpaceGuid"
00398|     PcdType  = "PcdsFixedAtBuild"
00399|     DataType  = "Uint32"
00400|     Value  = "$(MY_TOKEN_VALUE)"
00401|     TargetDSC = Yes
00402| End
00403| ```
00404| 
00405| ---
00406| 
00407| ## 8. ELINK Patterns
00408| 
00409| ### ELINK Chain Root (Owning Module)
00410| 
00411| ```sdl
00412| ELINK
00413|     Name  = "MyHandlerList"
00414|     InvokeOrder = ReplaceParent
00415| End
00416| ```
00417| 
00418| ### ELINK Registration (Contributor)
00419| 
00420| ```sdl
00421| ELINK
00422|     Name  = "MyHookFunction,"          # Note trailing comma
00423|     Parent  = "MyHandlerList"
00424|     InvokeOrder = AfterParent
00425| End
00426| ```
00427| 
00428| ### Function Typedef (Owning Module Header)
00429| 
00430| ```c
00431| typedef VOID (MY_HANDLER_HOOK) (UINT8 Data, UINT64 Parameter);
00432| ```
00433| 
00434| ### Consumption (Owning Module .c)
00435| 
00436| ```c
00437| #include <MyModuleELinks.h>
00438| 
00439| extern MY_HANDLER_HOOK MY_HANDLER_LIST EndOfList;
00440| MY_HANDLER_HOOK* MyHandlers[] = {MY_HANDLER_LIST NULL};
00441| ```
00442| 
00443| ---
00444| 
00445| ## 9. Customization Layers
00446| 
00447| Changes should target the **outermost applicable layer**:
00448| 
00449| ```
00450| ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
00451| │  <project>.sdl           (project overrides)    │
00452| ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
00453| │  <VendorPkg>/            (vendor/OEM custom)    │
00454| ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
00455| │  CrbPkg/ or AmiCrbPkg/   (board reference)      │
00456| ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
00457| │  AmiModulePkg/           (AMI core - modify OK) │
00458| ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
00459| │  Intel/                  (use AMI OVERRIDE)     │
00460| ├─────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
00461| │  MdePkg / MdeModulePkg   (use AMI OVERRIDE)     │
00462| └─────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
00463| ```
00464| 
00465| ### Rules
00466| - Prefer SDL token overrides over code patches
00467| - Use ELINK hooks instead of modifying original module source
00468| - Vendor package customizations override CRB package
00469| - Never modify `Build/` or `Conf/` files (auto-generated)
00470| 
00471| ---
00472| 
00473| ## 10. Module Type Specific Patterns
00474| 
00475| ### PEI Module Entry Point
00476| 
00477| ```c
00478| EFI_STATUS
00479| EFIAPI
00480| MyPeiInit (
00481|   IN       EFI_PEI_FILE_HANDLE  FileHandle,
00482|   IN CONST EFI_PEI_SERVICES     **PeiServices
00483|   )
00484| ```
00485| 
00486| ### DXE Driver Entry Point
00487| 
00488| ```c
00489| EFI_STATUS
00490| EFIAPI
00491| MyDxeInit (
00492|   IN EFI_HANDLE        ImageHandle,
00493|   IN EFI_SYSTEM_TABLE  *SystemTable
00494|   )
00495| ```
00496| 
00497| ### SMM Driver Entry Point
00498| 
00499| ```c
00500| EFI_STATUS
00501| EFIAPI
00502| MySmmInit (
00503|   IN EFI_HANDLE        ImageHandle,
00504|   IN EFI_SYSTEM_TABLE  *SystemTable
00505|   )
00506| ```
00507| 
00508| ---
00509| 
00510| ## 11. Common AMI-Specific Patterns
00511| 
00512| ### Setup Variable Access
00513| 
00514| ```c
00515| #include <SetupVariable.h>
00516| 
00517| SETUP_DATA  SetupData;
00518| UINTN       VariableSize = sizeof(SETUP_DATA);
00519| 
00520| Status = pRS->GetVariable(
00521|            L"Setup",
00522|            &gSetupGuid,
00523|            NULL,
00524|            &VariableSize,
00525|            &SetupData
00526|            );
00527| ```
00528| 
00529| ### Boot Script Saving
00530| 
00531| ```c
00532| #include <AmiCspLib.h>
00533| 
00534| BOOT_SCRIPT_S3_IO_WRITE_MACRO(
00535|   gBootScript,
00536|   EfiBootScriptWidthUint32,
00537|   IoAddress,
00538|   1,
00539|   &Value
00540|   );
00541| ```
00542| 
00543| ### NVRAM Read/Write
00544| 
00545| ```c
00546| #include <AmiDxeLib.h>
00547| 
00548| Status = pRS->SetVariable(
00549|            L"MyVariable",
00550|            &gMyVariableGuid,
00551|            EFI_VARIABLE_NON_VOLATILE | EFI_VARIABLE_BOOTSERVICE_ACCESS,
00552|            sizeof(MyData),
00553|            &MyData
00554|            );
00555| ```

00558| 
00559| ## Validation Checklist
00560| 
00561| When reviewing code for AMI organization standards compliance:
00562| 
00563| ☐ **File Header**
00564|   - [ ] AMI copyright banner present
00565|   - [ ] Current year in copyright
00566|   - [ ] `@file` block with correct filename and description
00567| 
00568| ☐ **File Organization**
00569|   - [ ] Sections in correct order (Include, Constant/Macro/Type, Variable/External)
00570|   - [ ] `//------` separators present
00571|   - [ ] Include order follows AMI pattern
00572| 
00573| ☐ **Naming**
00574|   - [ ] Functions use PascalCase
00575|   - [ ] Entry points use `<Module>Init` pattern
00576|   - [ ] Global variables use `g` prefix
00577|   - [ ] Constants use ALL_CAPS
00578|   - [ ] No raw C types (`int`, `char`, `void`)
00579| 
00580| ☐ **INF Structure**
00581|   - [ ] Sections in correct order
00582|   - [ ] `MdePkg/MdePkg.dec` first in `[Packages]`
00583|   - [ ] Entry-point library first in `[LibraryClasses]`
00584| 
00585| ☐ **Customization Layer**
00586|   - [ ] Changes in appropriate layer (outermost)
00587|   - [ ] AMI OVERRIDE markers used correctly (Intel/EDK2 only)
00588|   - [ ] SDL tokens preferred over code patches
00589| 
00590| ☐ **AMI Patterns**
00591|   - [ ] ELINK registrations have trailing comma
00592|   - [ ] Setup variable access uses proper pattern
00593|   - [ ] Boot script macros used correctly
00594|

00007| 
00008| # AMI Module Developer's Guide Standards Validation (MANDATORY)
00009| 
00010| ## Purpose
00011| Validate reviewed code against **mandatory** AMI Aptio 5.x Module Developer's Guide standards that extend beyond UEFI 2.10+, PI 1.8+, and EDK II C Coding Standard 2.2+ requirements.
00012| 
00013| **Compliance Level:** MANDATORY - Violations impact the final review verdict and may trigger CONDITIONAL APPROVE or BLOCK.
00014| 
00015| ## When to Use
00016| - **At the end of UEFI code review** (after Stage 3 verdict is written)
00017| - To check AMI-specific organization coding standards
00018| - Only on files that were already reviewed for UEFI/PI/EDK2 compliance
00019| 
00020| ## Scope
00021| This skill checks **AMI-specific mandatory** patterns only. UEFI/PI/EDK2 spec compliance is already covered by the main review stages.
00022| 
00023| ## Standards Reference
00024| See [AMI Module Developer Standards](./Module_Developer_Standards.md) for complete mandatory organization requirements.
00025| 
00026| ## Validation Procedure
00027| 
00028| ### Step 1: Identify Files in Scope
00029| Review only files that were checked in the main review (listed in `cc_registry.md` with Review Status ≠ `NA`). Do not review files that were skipped or excluded.
00030| 
00031| ### Step 2: Classify File Ownership (AMI vs Third-Party)
00032| 
00033| For each in-scope file, determine ownership by reading the file header (first 50 lines):
00034| 
00035| **Classification Rules:**
00036| 
00037| | Condition | Classification | Validation Scope |
00038| |-----------|----------------|------------------|
00039| | File contains AMI copyright banner (`Copyright (c) 1985-20XX, AMI`) | ✅ **AMI-owned** | Entire file |
00040| | File contains third-party copyright (Intel, Microsoft, EDK2, etc.) AND contains AMI override markers | ⚙️ **Third-party with AMI overrides** | AMI override sections only |
00041| | File contains NO copyright header | ✅ **AMI-owned** (default assumption) | Entire file |
00042| | File contains only third-party copyright, NO AMI override markers | ❌ **Third-party only** | Skip validation |
00043| 
00044| **AMI Override Marker Patterns (case-insensitive):**
00045| 
00046| Detect AMI-owned code sections in third-party files using these marker patterns:
00047| 
00048| | Start Marker | End Marker | Purpose |
00049| |--------------|------------|---------|
00050| | `// AMI OVERRIDE START` | `// AMI OVERRIDE END` | General EDK2/Intel overrides |
00051| | `//APTIOV_SERVER_OVERRIDE_START:` | `//APTIOV_SERVER_OVERRIDE_END:` | Server platform overrides |
00052| | `//APTIOV_SERVER_OVERRIDE_RC_START:` | `//APTIOV_SERVER_OVERRIDE_RC_END:` | Server platform RC overrides |
00053| | Any marker matching: `//<PREFIX>OVERRIDE*START` where PREFIX contains "AMI" or "APTIOV" | Corresponding `END` marker | Project-specific AMI override tags |
00054| 
00055| **Detection Pattern:**
00056| ```regex
00057| Start: ^\s*//\s*(AMI|APTIOV)[A-Z_]*OVERRIDE[A-Z_]*START
00058| End:   ^\s*//\s*(AMI|APTIOV)[A-Z_]*OVERRIDE[A-Z_]*END
00059| ```
00060| 
00061| **Copyright Detection Patterns:**
00062| - AMI: `Copyright (c) 1985-YYYY, AMI` or `Copyright.*AMI`
00063| - Intel: `Copyright.*Intel Corporation`
00064| - Microsoft: `Copyright.*Microsoft Corporation`
00065| - EDK2/TianoCore: `Copyright.*TianoCore`
00066| 
00067| ### Step 3: Extract AMI Code Sections
00068| 
00069| For each file, extract the relevant code based on classification:
00070| 
00071| **AMI-owned files (entire file validation):**
00072| - Read full file content
00073| - Validate all sections against AMI organization standards
00074| - Apply all checks from Step 4
00075| 
00076| **Third-party files with AMI overrides (section-only validation):**
00077| - Parse file line-by-line
00078| - Extract ONLY lines between AMI override START/END markers (inclusive)
00079| - Validate extracted sections only
00080| - Report findings with original file line numbers
00081| - Record: `[Third-party file — validating AMI override sections only]`
00082| 
00083| **Third-party files without AMI code:**
00084| - Skip validation entirely
00085| - Record: `[SKIP: Third-party file, no AMI code sections detected]`
00086| 
00087| **Examples:**
00088| 
00089| ```c
00090| // Intel copyright header
00091| // ...
00092| void IntelFunction() {
00093|   // Skip this - third-party code
00094| }
00095| 
00096| // AMI OVERRIDE START — Add custom validation
00097| BOOLEAN gAmiCustomFlag = FALSE;  // ← Validate this (AMI code)
00098| 
00099| EFI_STATUS ValidateCustom() {   // ← Validate this (AMI code)
00100|   return EFI_SUCCESS;
00101| }
00102| // AMI OVERRIDE END
00103| 
00104| void AnotherIntelFunction() {
00105|   // Skip this - third-party code
00106| }
00107| ```
00108| 
00109| ### Step 4: Check Organization Standards
00110| 
00111| For each AMI code section (entire file OR extracted override blocks), verify:
00112| 
00113| 1. **File Header Format** (AMI-owned files only)
00114|    - AMI copyright banner present and current year (2026)
00115|    - Doxygen `@file` block with brief description
00116|    - Skip for third-party files with overrides (header is third-party)
00117| 
00118| 2. **Source File Organization** (all AMI code)
00119|    - Sections in correct order with `//------` separators (AMI files only)
00120|    - Include order follows AMI pattern (when includes are in AMI section)
00121| 
00122| 3. **Naming Conventions** (all AMI code) - MANDATORY
00123|    - Functions: PascalCase
00124|    - Entry points: `<Module>Init` pattern
00125|    - Global variables: `g` prefix + PascalCase
00126|    - Registration variables: `g<Name>Reg` pattern
00127|    - Constants/Macros: ALL_CAPS
00128|    - **No raw C types** (`int`, `char`, `void`) - **MANDATORY-MAJOR violation if present**
00129| 
00130| 4. **INF File Structure** (AMI-owned INF files only)
00131|    - Sections in AMI-specified order
00132|    - `MdePkg/MdePkg.dec` listed first in `[Packages]`
00133|    - Skip for third-party INF files
00134| 
00135| 5. **AMI-Specific Patterns** (all AMI code)
00136|    - SDL token usage
00137|    - ELINK patterns (when modified)
00138|    - Customization layer compliance
00139| 
00140| ### Step 5: Report Findings
00141| 
00142| Append findings to the review report under a new section. Distinguish file classification in reporting:
00143| 
00144| ```markdown
00145| ## AMI Module Developer's Guide Standards (MANDATORY)
00146| 
00147| Files validated against AMI Aptio 5.x Module Developer's Guide mandatory standards (`.github/skills/ami-module-developer-standards/`).
00148| 
00149| ### Summary
00150| - AMI-owned files validated: X
00151| - Third-party files with AMI overrides validated: Y
00152| - Third-party files skipped (no AMI code): Z
00153| 
00154| ### Findings
00155| 
00156| **[AmiModulePkg/Nvram/NvramDxe.c]** (AMI-owned)
00157| - File Header: Missing current year in copyright (2025 vs 2026)
00158|   - Location: Line 3
00159|   - Severity: **MANDATORY-MINOR** (impacts verdict score)
00160|   - Fix: Update copyright year to 2026
00161| - Raw C Type: `int` used instead of UEFI type
00162|   - Location: Line 147
00163|   - Severity: **MANDATORY-MAJOR** (blocking - ABI incompatibility risk)
00164|   - Fix: Replace `int bufferSize` with `UINT32 BufferSize`
00165| 
00166| **[Intel/ClientOneSiliconPkg/Cpu/LibraryPrivate/PeiCpuPolicyLib/PeiCpuPolicyLib.c]** (Third-party with AMI overrides)
00167| - [Third-party file — validating AMI override sections only: lines 245-260, 312-340]
00168| - Naming Convention: Global variable missing `g` prefix
00169|   - Location: Line 247 (inside `//APTIOV_SERVER_OVERRIDE_START:` block)
00170|   - Severity: **MANDATORY-MINOR** (impacts verdict score)
00171|   - Fix: Rename `AmiCustomBuffer` to `gAmiCustomBuffer`
00172| 
00173| **[Intel/ServerSiliconPkg/Mem/Library/MemRcLib/MemRc.c]** (Third-party only)
00174| - [SKIP: Third-party file, no AMI code sections detected]
00175| 
00176| ### Module Developer Standards Verdict
00177| ✅ **COMPLIANT** / ⚠️ **NON-COMPLIANT (impacts main verdict)**
00178| 
00179| **Severity Impact on Main Review Verdict:**
00180| - **MANDATORY-MAJOR**: Added to main review MAJOR count → may trigger CONDITIONAL APPROVE or BLOCK
00181| - **MANDATORY-MINOR**: Added to main review MINOR count → impacts score calculation
00182| - **INFO**: Informational only, no verdict impact
00183| 
00184| > **Note:** Module Developer Guide violations are **MANDATORY** and affect the main review verdict (APPROVE/CONDITIONAL/BLOCK). All MANDATORY-MAJOR findings must be fixed before merge. MANDATORY-MINOR findings contribute to the overall quality score.
00185| ```
00186| 
00187| **Reporting Guidelines:**
00188| 
00189| 1. **Group by file classification:**
00190|    - AMI-owned files first
00191|    - Third-party files with AMI overrides second
00192|    - Skipped third-party files last
00193| 
00194| 2. **For AMI override sections:**
00195|    - Always include line range of override block in brackets: `[Third-party file — validating AMI override sections only: lines X-Y, Z-W]`
00196|    - Report finding location as absolute line number in original file
00197|    - Note which override block contains the finding
00198| 
00199| 3. **For skipped files:**
00200|    - Use single-line skip notation: `[SKIP: Third-party file, no AMI code sections detected]`
00201|    - Do not open findings for pure third-party code
00202| 
00203| 4. **If no findings:**
00204|    - Write: `> All reviewed files comply with AMI organization coding standards.`
00205|    - Still list skipped third-party files in summary
00206| 
00207| ### Step 6: Impact on Main Review Verdict
00208| - **DOES impact** the main review verdict (APPROVE/CONDITIONAL/BLOCK)
00209| - Module Developer Guide violations are **MANDATORY**
00210| - **MANDATORY-MAJOR** findings:
00211|   - Added to main review MAJOR count
00212|   - May change verdict from APPROVE → CONDITIONAL or BLOCK
00213|   - Must be fixed before merge
00214| - **MANDATORY-MINOR** findings:
00215|   - Added to main review MINOR count
00216|   - Contribute to quality score (affects verdict thresholds)
00217| - **Verdict recalculation:** If new MAJOR findings trigger verdict change, update `Result.txt` and append note to main verdict section
00218| - **AMI code only:** Only AMI-owned files and AMI override sections in third-party files are validated
00219| - **Third-party code skipped:** Pure third-party code (Intel, EDK2, Microsoft) is not validated
00220| - **Default assumption:** Files without copyright headers are treated as AMI-owned
00221| 
00222| ## File Classification Examples
00223| 
00224| ### Example 1: AMI-owned file
00225| ```c
00226| //***********************************************************************
00227| //*                                                                     *
00228| //*                 Copyright (c) 1985-2026, AMI.                       *
00229| //*                                                                     *
00230| //***********************************************************************
00231| /** @file NvramDxe.c
00232|     NVRAM driver implementation
00233| **/
00234| ```
00235| **Classification:** ✅ AMI-owned → Validate entire file
00236| 
00237| ### Example 2: Third-party with AMI overrides
00238| ```c
00239| /** @file
00240|   Copyright (c) 2020 - 2024, Intel Corporation. All rights reserved.
00241|   SPDX-License-Identifier: BSD-2-Clause-Patent
00242| **/
00243| 
00244| void IntelFunction() {
00245|   // Third-party code - skip
00246| }
00247| 
00248| // AMI OVERRIDE START — Add custom initialization
00249| EFI_STATUS AmiCustomInit() {
00250|   // AMI code - validate this section only
00251| }
00252| // AMI OVERRIDE END
00253| 
00254| void AnotherIntelFunction() {
00255|   // Third-party code - skip
00256| }
00257| ```
00258| **Classification:** ⚙️ Third-party with AMI overrides → Validate override sections only (lines between START/END markers)
00259| 
00260| ### Example 3: Third-party without AMI code
00261| ```c
00262| /** @file
00263|   Copyright (c) 2020 - 2024, Intel Corporation. All rights reserved.
00264|   SPDX-License-Identifier: BSD-2-Clause-Patent
00265| **/
00266| 
00267| void IntelFunction() {
00268|   // No AMI override markers found
00269| }
00270| ```
00271| **Classification:** ❌ Third-party only → Skip validation
00272| 
00273| ### Example 4: No copyright (assume AMI)
00274| ```c
00275| #include <Uefi.h>
00276| 
00277| EFI_STATUS MyFunction() {
00278|   // No copyright header found
00279| }
00280| ```
00281| **Classification:** ✅ AMI-owned (default) → Validate entire file
00282| 
00283| ## Override Marker Variations
00284| 
00285| All these patterns are recognized as AMI override markers:
00286| 
00287| ```c
00288| // AMI OVERRIDE START
00289| // AMI OVERRIDE START — Add validation
00290| // AMI OVERRIDE START: Fix memory leak
00291| //AMI OVERRIDE START
00292| //  AMI OVERRIDE START  
00293| 
00294| //APTIOV_SERVER_OVERRIDE_START:
00295| //APTIOV_SERVER_OVERRIDE_RC_START:
00296| // APTIOV_SERVER_OVERRIDE_START: Description
00297| 
00298| // AMI_CUSTOM_OVERRIDE_START:
00299| // AMI_PROJECT_OVERRIDE_START
00300| ```
00301| 
00302| **Detection is case-insensitive and whitespace-tolerant.**
00303| 
00304| ## Severity Classification Guidelines
00305| 
00306| ### MANDATORY-MAJOR (Blocking)
00307| - Raw C types (`int`, `char`, `void`, `unsigned`) in UEFI code
00308| - Missing AMI OVERRIDE markers in Intel/EDK2 file modifications
00309| - Wrong customization layer (modifying Intel files without markers)
00310| - Missing `g` prefix on global variables in security-critical code
00311| - Entry point not following `<Module>Init` pattern
00312| 
00313| ### MANDATORY-MINOR (Score Impact)
00314| - Copyright year outdated
00315| - Function naming case incorrect (non-PascalCase)
00316| - File organization sections out of order
00317| - Include order not following AMI pattern
00318| - Missing `@file` Doxygen block
00319| - INF section order incorrect
00320| 
00321| ### INFO (Tracking Only)
00322| - Suggestions for code clarity
00323| - Optional improvements
00324| - Style preferences
00325| 
00326| ## Progressive Loading
00327| The skill loads lightweight validation logic first, then references [Module_Developer_Standards.md](./Module_Developer_Standards.md) only when detailed standard text is needed for citation.
